<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that every complex K3 surface <i>X</i> is Oka, resolving the K3 Oka conjecture. Equivalently, for every <i>m</i> ≥ 1, every holomorphic map to <i>X</i> from a neighborhood of a compact convex set in ℂ<sup><i>m</i></sup> can be approximated uniformly on that set by entire maps $`\mathbb C^m\to X`$.

- **claim-main-cap-k3** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $X$ be a complex K3 surface, with any smooth Hermitian distance $d_X$. For every integer $m\geq1$, every nonempty compact convex set $K\subset\mathbb C^m$, every open neighborhood $U$ of $K$, every holomorphic map $f:U\to X$, and every $\epsilon>0$, there exists a holomorphic map $F:\mathbb C^m\to X$ such that $$\sup_{z\in K}d_X(F(z),f(z))<\epsilon.$$
  - Explanation: Every complex K3 surface has the convex approximation property in every source dimension: holomorphic maps near a compact convex set are uniformly approximable by entire maps.
- **claim-dense-entire-immersion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-dense)): Let $S$ be a complex K3 surface. For every $x\in S$ and every nonzero vector $v\in T_xS$, there is a holomorphic immersion $f:\mathbb C\to S$ such that $$f(0)=x,\qquad f'(0)=v,\qquad \overline{f(\mathbb C)}=S,$$ where closure is taken in the ordinary complex topology. If $S$ is projective, then $f(\mathbb C)$ is Zariski dense.
  - Explanation: Through every point and tangent vector of a K3 surface there is an entire immersion with dense image, Zariski dense in the projective case.
- **claim-two-complete-directions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-criterion)): Suppose there are holomorphic maps $$\sigma_1:\mathbb C\times\Delta_b\longrightarrow X,\qquad \sigma_2:\Delta_b\times\mathbb C\longrightarrow X$$ which are locally biholomorphic at $(0,0)$ and satisfy $\sigma_1(z,0)=\sigma_2(z,0)$ for small $z$. If $\sigma_2$ is locally biholomorphic at $(0,c)$, then $L$ holds at $x=\sigma_2(0,c)$.* *The same conclusion holds for a sequence of pairs whose long coordinate domains are discs of radii tending to infinity, whose bounded-coordinate radii are bounded below, and whose maps converge near $(0,0)$ and, for the second map, near $(0,c)$ to the above nondegenerate germs. Exact axis agreement is required for every pair. In this version $x$ is the value of the limiting second germ at $(0,c)$.
  - Explanation: Two transverse complete strip maps with exact agreement on an axis give property L at the displaced point.
- **lemma-planar-pieces** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-pieces)): Given a special pair $D\subset D'$ and an open neighborhood $N$ of $D$, there are compact sets $A,B_0,B_1\subset\mathbb C$ and bounded open sets $U_A,U_0,U_1$ such that $$\begin{gathered} \overline{U_A}\subset\operatorname{int}A,\qquad \overline{U_i}\subset\operatorname{int}B_i\quad(i=0,1),\\ D\subset U_0,\qquad D'\subset U_A\cup U_0\cup U_1,\\ A\cap D=\varnothing,\qquad B_0\cap B_1=\varnothing,\qquad B_0\subset N. \end{gathered}$$ The compact sets $D\cup A$ and $B_0\cup B_1$ are polynomially convex. For $U_B=U_0\cup U_1$, the two closed fringes $\overline{U_A\setminus U_B}$ and $\overline{U_B\setminus U_A}$ have positive distance.
  - Explanation: An attached-disc pair is decomposed into compact and open planar pieces with separated fringes and polynomially convex unions.
- **lemma-planar-splitting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-splitting)): Let $U_A,U_B\subset\mathbb C$ be bounded open sets with positively separated closed fringes, and put $C=U_A\cap U_B$. For every open parameter polydisc $P$ and every $k$, there are bounded linear operators $$\begin{aligned} R_A&:H^\infty(C\times P,\mathbb C^k)\longrightarrow H^\infty(U_A\times P,\mathbb C^k),\\ R_B&:H^\infty(C\times P,\mathbb C^k)\longrightarrow H^\infty(U_B\times P,\mathbb C^k) \end{aligned}$$ satisfying $R_Au-R_Bu=u$. Their bounds depend only on the planar sets and not on $P$ or its dimension.
  - Explanation: Additive Cousin splitting operators on a planar overlap with bounds independent of the passive parameters.
- **remark-alignment** (construction, [source](#stmt-loc-alignment)): Two strip maps locally biholomorphic at chosen meeting preimages, with transverse central curves there, can be put in the form of Proposition <a href="#loc:criterion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="loc:criterion">4</a>. Locally lift the first central curve through the inverse of the second strip, writing the lift as $(a(z),t(z))$, where its bounded coordinate is $a$. Replace the second strip by $\widetilde\sigma_2(z,w)=\sigma_2(a(z),t(z)+w)$. Transversality says $a'(0)\ne0$, and time zero gives exact axis agreement. If the original second central curve also passes through $x$ and its strip is regular there, some finite $c$ gives a regular chart $\widetilde\sigma_2(0,c)=x$. For finite-length families whose central germs converge to transverse germs through a common point, the implicit function theorem first gives nearby intersection points and meeting preimages. Translate the preimages to the origin and apply the same construction. Convergence and transversality give a uniformly positive bounded-coordinate radius; the translations and the function $t$ lose only a bounded amount of long-coordinate length. Thus this construction also supplies the aligned families in the finite-length part of the proposition.
  - Explanation: Two strip maps with transverse central curves can be reparametrized into the aligned form required by the two-direction criterion.
- **lemma-local-chart-sprays** (construction, [source](#stmt-loc-spray)): Let $f:S\to X$ be holomorphic on a Stein manifold, let $K\Subset S$ be compact, and assume $f^*TX$ is holomorphically trivial on $S$. After shrinking the source neighborhood there is a holomorphic map $f^\sharp(z,v)$ for $z$ near $K$ and $v\in\Delta_r^2$, with $f^\sharp(z,0)=f(z)$, such that $v\mapsto f^\sharp(z,v)$ is injective with invertible differential. The hypothesis on $f^*TX$ holds for a contractible Stein source and for a planar domain times a polydisc.
  - Explanation: Around the graph of a holomorphic map on a Stein source with trivial pulled-back tangent bundle there is a chart spray.
- **lemma-deformation-stein** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-deformation)): Let $\pi:\mathcal X\to B$ be a holomorphic family, and let $f:S\to\mathcal X_{b_0}$ be holomorphic from a Stein manifold with image in the submersion locus of $\pi$. On every relatively compact source domain there is a holomorphic family of maps $f_b$ into $\mathcal X_b$ for $b$ near $b_0$, with $f_{b_0}=f$.
  - Explanation: Maps into the submersion locus deform along a holomorphic family on relatively compact source domains.
- **lemma-planar-spray-gluing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-gluing)): Suppose two holomorphic sprays over bounded planar domains $U_A,U_B$ and a parameter polydisc are uniformly close on their overlap, with a positive margin in an auxiliary spray polydisc. Suppose the first spray is a chart spray there with uniform inverse-chart margins, and the closed planar fringes are positively separated. If the closeness is sufficiently small, their central maps can be glued after arbitrarily small holomorphic corrections in the auxiliary variables. The glued map is arbitrarily close to the first central map on any prescribed compact subset of its domain on which that spray is fixed, or has a uniform modulus of continuity in its auxiliary variables.
  - Explanation: Two close planar sprays with separated fringes can be glued after small corrections in the auxiliary variables.
- **prop-planar-enlargement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-planar)): Let $K$ be a closed circular disc of radius $r$, or a closed circular annulus with outer radius $r$, and let $f$ be holomorphic near $K\times P$, where $P$ is a closed polydisc. Suppose that for every $\zeta$ on the outer circle there is a coordinate witness neighborhood $V_\zeta$ for $L$ containing $f(\{\zeta\}\times P)$. For every $R>r$ and $P_0\Subset\operatorname{int}P$, one can approximate $f$ uniformly on $K\times P_0$ by a map holomorphic near the enlarged disc, respectively annulus with the same inner radius and outer radius $R$, times $P_0$.
  - Explanation: A disc or annulus is enlarged with passive parameters provided witness neighborhoods exist along the outer circle.
- **cor-propagation-small-disc** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-disc)): Suppose a coordinate neighborhood contains a closed affine complex-line disc centered at $x$, with its boundary in the open locus where $L$ holds. Then $L$ holds at $x$. In particular, if the complement of that locus is contained locally in a proper analytic subset, $L$ holds everywhere.
  - Explanation: Property L propagates from the boundary of a small affine disc to its center, and across proper analytic exceptional sets.
- **remark-uniform-discs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-auto-remark-1)): The proof also records a useful uniform version: finitely many coordinate discs whose boundaries lie in fixed compact subsets of the good locus retain the required boundary containment after sufficiently small deformations of the coordinate charts. All arguments in this section concern one planar variable with passive parameters. No approximation theorem for arbitrary multidimensional source compacta has been used.
  - Explanation: Boundary containment for finitely many coordinate discs persists under small chart deformations; only one planar variable with passive parameters is used.
- **thm-L-implies-cap** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-ana-cap)): Let $Y$ be a complex surface with a complete compatible distance. If the local operation $L$ holds at every point of $Y$, then $Y$ has the convex approximation property in every source dimension.
  - Explanation: If property L holds at every point of a complex surface with a complete compatible distance, the surface has the convex approximation property in every source dimension.
- **lemma-open-overlap-gluing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-glue-open)): In the preceding buffered open-overlap geometry, let two holomorphic sprays into a complex manifold be related on their overlap by a fiber coordinate change $$\gamma(z,v)=v+c(z,v),\qquad v\in B_b.$$ Suppose $\\|c\\|_\infty$ can be made arbitrarily small with $b>0$ fixed. After any prescribed positive loss of base and fiber margins, the two sprays admit fiber reparametrizations close to the identity which make them agree. Evaluating at $v=0$ gives a glued holomorphic map. It is arbitrarily close to the original map on each compactum where the corresponding input spray is fixed, or has a uniform modulus of continuity in the fiber variable. No such uniform target estimate is asserted on a side whose spray varies without this control.
  - Explanation: Small nonlinear gluing of sprays on a buffered open overlap by a quadratically convergent iteration.
- **lemma-concentration-automorphism** (construction, [source](#stmt-ana-concentration)): Fix $m\geq2$, numbers $$1<r_m<\cdots<r_2<r_1, \qquad 0<a<1,\qquad r_j<a r_{j-1}\quad(2\leq j\leq m).$$ For an integer $d\geq2$, set $$P_j(w)=(w/r_j)^d,\qquad H_d(x)=\bigl(a x_m,P_2(x_2)-a x_1,\ldots, P_m(x_m)-a x_{m-1}\bigr).$$ There are constants $C\geq1$, $q\in(0,1)$, and $d_0$, depending only on the displayed fixed data, with the following properties. For $d\geq d_0$, $N\geq1$, and $$E_{d,N}=H_d^{-N}(\overline\Delta_1^{\,m}),$$ one has $$\label{ana:eq:power-bound} \max_{j\geq2}\|P_j(x_j)\|\leq C\max(1,\|x_1\|) \qquad(x\in E_{d,N}).$$ On $E_{d,N}\cap\{\|x_1\|>r_1\}$, all $x_j$ are nonzero and $$\label{ana:eq:ratio-bound} \left\|\frac{H_d(x)_j}{a x_{j-1}}\right\|\leq q \qquad(2\leq j\leq m).$$ Consequently the holomorphic functions $$\xi_j(x)=\log\left(\frac{P_j(x_j)}{a x_{j-1}}\right), \qquad 2\leq j\leq m,$$ using the logarithm on $\{\|\zeta-1\|<1\}$, satisfy $$\|\xi_j(x)\|\leq B:=-\log(1-q).$$ Here the assertions about holomorphicity are on any open inverse image of the unit polydisc; all the estimates also hold on its closure. With $$D=d^{m-1},\qquad \rho_1=1,\qquad \rho_j=r_j(a\rho_{j-1})^{1/d},$$ there is a single-valued holomorphic function $s$ on this end such that $$\label{ana:eq:parameterization} x_j=\rho_j s^{d^{m-j}} \exp\left(\sum_{k=2}^j\frac{\xi_k}{d^{j-k+1}}\right), \qquad 1\leq j\leq m.$$ For $j=1$, the sum is empty and this says $x_1=s^D$.
  - Explanation: A polynomial automorphism of high degree concentrates the outer part of a polydisc into one long planar coordinate and bounded transverse coordinates.
- **prop-polydisc-approximation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ana-polydisc)): Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#ana:cap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ana:cap">14</a>, a map holomorphic near a closed polydisc in $\mathbb C^m$ can be approximated uniformly there by entire maps $\mathbb C^m\to Y$. More precisely, a map near $\overline\Delta_1^{\,m}$ can be approximated there by maps holomorphic near $\overline\Delta_R^{\,m}$, for every finite $R>1$.
  - Explanation: Under the hypotheses of the globalization theorem, maps near a closed polydisc are approximable by entire maps.
- **lemma-complexify-real-parameter** (theorem, [source](#stmt-glue-interval)): Let $I\subset\mathbb R$ be a compact interval and let $C\subset\mathbb C^k$ be a compact convex set. Suppose $H_s(q)$ is a smooth family, for $s$ in a neighborhood of $I$, of holomorphic maps on a common neighborhood of $C$, with values in a complex surface. Smoothness is understood on a common neighborhood with all finite derivatives under consideration. For every finite $j$ and every $\epsilon>0$, after an arbitrarily small loss of the specified compact margins, $H$ can be approximated in $C^j(I\times C)$ by a map holomorphic on a neighborhood of $I\times C$ in $\mathbb C\times\mathbb C^k$. The size of this complex neighborhood may depend on the approximant. Additional closed polydisc factors can be included in $C$, with fixed larger neighborhoods.
  - Explanation: A smooth real-parameter family of holomorphic maps is approximated by a jointly holomorphic map near the interval.
- **lemma-parameter-disc** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ana-parameter-disc)): Assume ${\cal A}_r$. Let $I\subset\mathbb R$ be a compact interval, $C\subset\mathbb C^r$ compact and convex, and $P$ a closed polydisc. A map holomorphic near $I\times C\times P$ can be approximated there, with any prescribed finite derivative accuracy and smaller positive margins, by entire maps of all the displayed complex variables.
  - Explanation: Granted the induction hypothesis, maps near an interval times a convex set and polydisc are approximated by entire maps in all variables.
- **lemma-convex-real-face** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ana-face)): Assume ${\cal A}_{n-1}$, where $n\geq1$, and Proposition <a href="#ana:polydisc" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ana:polydisc">17</a>. Let $F\subset\mathbb R\times\mathbb C^{n-1}$ be compact and convex, and let $P$ be a closed polydisc. Every holomorphic map near $F\times P$ can be approximated on $F\times P$ by entire maps on $\mathbb C^n\times\mathbb C^{\dim P}$, with any fixed finite derivative accuracy along the real face and smaller positive margins.
  - Explanation: Maps near a compact convex real face are approximated by entire maps with tangential derivative control.
- **lemma-linear-trace-splitting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-glue-linear-trace)): There are $C>0$ and an integer $p\geq1$ with the following property. For $0<h<\min(r/2,1)$ and a vector-valued function $u$ on $M_r\times B_b$ with the regularity just specified, there are linear operators producing functions $a^\pm$, holomorphic on $Q_{r-h}^\pm\times B_b$ and continuous up to $M_{r-h}\times B_b$, such that $$a^+-a^-=u\quad\hbox{on }M_{r-h}\times B_b, \qquad \\|a^+\\|_{r-h,b,*}+\\|a^-\\|_{r-h,b,*} \leq Ch^{-p}\sup_{v\in B_b}\\|u(\,\cdot\,,v)\\|_{C^\alpha(M_r)}.$$ The operators preserve holomorphic dependence on all auxiliary variables.
  - Explanation: Additive splitting of a Hölder jump across a real interface with polynomial loss in the margin.
- **lemma-trace-gluing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-glue-trace)): The conclusion of Lemma <a href="#glue:open" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="glue:open">15</a> holds for sprays on $Q_r^-$ and $Q_r^+$ when their transition is given on $M_r\times B_b$ and is arbitrarily small in $$\\|c\\|_{r,b,\alpha} =\sup_{v\in B_b}\\|c(\,\cdot\,,v)\\|_{C^\alpha(M_r)}.$$ Here the sprays are holomorphic on the open sides, continuous up to the interface, and the transition is holomorphic in $(z',v)$ there. The resulting glued map is holomorphic across the interface. No lower bound is assumed for a complex normal thickness on which the input transition is small.
  - Explanation: Nonlinear gluing of sprays across a real interface needs only smallness of the transition in a Hölder trace norm.
- **prop-projective-strips** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prj-strips)): Let $S$ be a smooth projective complex K3 surface. There is a nonempty Zariski open subset $U\subset S$ such that, for every $x\in U$, there are $b>0$ and holomorphic maps $$\sigma_1:\mathbb C\times\Delta_b\longrightarrow S,\qquad \sigma_2:\Delta_b\times\mathbb C\longrightarrow S$$ which are locally biholomorphic at the origin and satisfy $$\sigma_1(0,0)=\sigma_2(0,0)=x,\qquad \sigma_1(z,0)=\sigma_2(z,0)\quad (\|z\|<b).$$ The complement $S\setminus U$ is algebraic of dimension at most one. Consequently $S$ has property $L$ everywhere.
  - Explanation: On a projective K3 surface two transverse complete strips exist at every point of a Zariski open set, giving property L everywhere.
- **lemma-prepare-genus-one-families** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prj-prepare)): Let $S$ be a smooth projective integral surface, let $H$ be an ample divisor, and let $p:Y\to T$ be a smooth proper family of connected genus-one curves over a smooth irreducible algebraic curve. Suppose that $q:Y\to S$ has nonconstant source-curve moduli and that $q_{t_0}$ is the normalization map of an integral curve $C\subset S$. After removing finitely many points of $T$, every $q_t$ normalizes an integral image curve $C_t$ with $H\cdot C_t=H\cdot C$. The evaluation map $q$ is dominant and generically finite, and some nonempty Zariski open $V\subset S$ has finite etale surjective inverse image map $q^{-1}(V)\to V$.
  - Explanation: Families of genus-one curves with nonconstant moduli have dominant, generically finite evaluation maps, finite etale over a Zariski open set.
- **lemma-transverse-lifts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prj-transverse)): There is a nonempty Zariski open $U\subset V$ such that any two lifts $y_i\in Y_i^V$ of a point $x\in U$ have distinct transported vertical tangent lines $$dq_1\bigl(\ker(dp_1)_{y_1}\bigr) \ne dq_2\bigl(\ker(dp_2)_{y_2}\bigr) \quad\text{in }T_xS.$$ The closure in $S$ of the exceptional locus has dimension at most one.
  - Explanation: Vertical directions of two families of different degrees are distinct over a Zariski open set.
- **lemma-complete-vertical-flows** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prj-flow)): Let $p:Y\to\Delta$ be a proper holomorphic submersion with connected genus-one fibers. After shrinking the base disc, there is a nowhere-zero holomorphic vertical vector field whose flow is jointly holomorphic for all complex times. Its restriction to the central fiber may be prescribed as any nonzero holomorphic vector field there.
  - Explanation: A proper genus-one submersion over a small disc carries a vertical field whose flow is complete for all complex times.
- **thm-annular-chain-sewing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sew-chain)): Let $M$ be a complex surface with a nowhere-zero holomorphic two-form $\omega$. Fix $0<h<1/8$, $r>0$, and $A<\infty$. For each $j\in\mathbb Z$, let $L_j\geq1$ and let $$\phi_j:\{ -2h<Y<L_j+2h\}\times\Delta_r\longrightarrow M$$ be a holomorphic map with $\phi_j^*\omega=\omega_0$. Suppose there are holomorphic translations $$J_j(w,p)=(w+a_j(p),p),\qquad \|\operatorname{Im}a_j(p)+L_j\|<h/8,\qquad \|a_j'(p)\|\leq A,$$ and exact symplectic transitions $F_j$ on $$S_j(h,r)=\{\|Y-L_j\|<h\}\times\Delta_r$$ such that $\phi_j=\phi_{j+1}\circ F_j$. Write $$J_j^{-1}F_j=(w+u_j,p+v_j).$$ Assume these lifts exist and $\sup_j\\|(u_j,v_j)\\|_{S_j(h,r)}\leq e$.* *There are $b>0$ and $e_*>0$, depending only on $h,r,A$ and the indicated margins, such that, if $e<e_*$, these charts give a holomorphic symplectic immersion $$\Phi:\mathbb C\times\Delta_b\longrightarrow M$$ which is periodic of period one in the first variable. On a smaller domain in every original block, with fixed positive margins at both ends, $\Phi$ is obtained from $\phi_j$ by a small periodic symplectic change of variables. The corrected transitions are $$(w,p)\longmapsto(w+\widetilde a_j(p),p).$$ The changes of variables and $\widetilde a_j-a_j$, together with any fixed finite number of derivatives on further smaller domains, tend uniformly to zero as $e\to0$. None of these assertions requires an upper bound for the $L_j$ or a bound on the number of blocks.
  - Explanation: An infinite chain of nearly matching exact symplectic blocks sews into one periodic symplectic immersion, with bounds independent of block lengths and number.
- **lemma-remove-zero-mode** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sew-linearize)): Suppose $E=(w+u,p+v)$ is exact symplectic on a seam and $\\|(u,v)\\|\leq e$. After a margin loss $\delta$ there are a holomorphic function $u_0(p)$ and a periodic mean-zero function $H$ such that $$(u,v)=(u_0,0)+X_H+R,\qquad X_H=(H_p,-H_w),\qquad \\|H\\|\leq Ce,\quad \\|R\\|\leq C\delta^{-2}e^2.$$ Here $u_0$ is the zero Fourier coefficient of $u$.
  - Explanation: An exact symplectic seam error splits into a translation, a Hamiltonian part and a quadratic remainder.
- **lemma-chain-splitting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sew-split)): Suppose the periodic functions $H_j$ have mean zero, are holomorphic on $S_j(s,\rho)$, and have norm at most $e$. Assume $$\|\operatorname{Im}a_j+L_j\|\leq\kappa<1/4.$$ There are periodic holomorphic functions $G_j$ on $D_j(s-\delta,\rho)$ satisfying $$\label{sew:split-equation} G_j-G_{j+1}\circ J_j=-H_j.$$ on the smaller seams. Their bounds, on domains with the appropriate additional losses, are $$\\|G_j\\|\leq C\delta^{-1}e,\qquad \\|X_{G_j}\\|\leq C\delta^{-2}e,\qquad \\|DX_{G_j}\\|\leq C\delta^{-3}e.$$ All constants are uniform in the block lengths and the number of blocks.
  - Explanation: Fourier splitting along the whole chain gives Hamiltonians with uniform bounds.
- **lemma-period-adjustment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sew-centers)): Suppose symplectically normalized charts, together with corresponding model charts, are available for centers varying in larger transverse discs. Suppose the actual charts are uniformly close to their models, and transitions at matching model positions are uniformly close to degree-one shears. The transition into a newly added block can be made exact by a transverse translation of that block of size bounded by its transition error. Recenter its model by the same translation. The approximation of that block by its own recentered model retains its original bound.* *Consequently, such adjustments can be repeated indefinitely whenever the updated centers remain in a region with uniform buffered chart choices. No bound on their accumulated displacement relative to the first center is required.
  - Explanation: A transverse translation of a new block makes its transition exact, repeatably, without bounds on accumulated displacement.
- **prop-open-period-region** (theorem, [source](#stmt-deg-open)): There is a nonempty open subset of the full marked K3 period domain such that every surface with period in this subset has property $L$ at every point.
  - Explanation: A nonempty open set of marked K3 periods consists of surfaces having property L everywhere.
- **lemma-regular-pencils** (theorem, [source](#stmt-deg-regular-pencils)): The quadrics and $G$ can be chosen so that:* 1. *Through every point of $E$ on each component there is a regular fiber of one of these finitely many pencils.* 2. *The union of their regular fibers contains the complement of a proper algebraic subset of each component.* 3. *Every regular fiber has, away from $E$, a transverse intersection with a regular fiber of another pencil.
  - Explanation: For suitable quadrics and quartic, finitely many pencils of rational curves have regular fibers through every point of the double curve with transverse partners.
- **lemma-core-coordinates** (theorem, [source](#stmt-deg-core-coordinates)): On a small parameter disc of any compact regular family there are coordinates $(z,p)\in\mathbb P^1\times\Delta_r$ in which the two ends are $z=0,\infty$ and $$\omega_\pm=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\frac{dz}{z}\wedge dp.$$ Either end can be designated as the entrance. With entrance at $\infty$, exit at $0$, and $z=c\exp(2\pi i w)$, this reads $\omega_\pm=dw\wedge dp$. The parameter $p$ is a local affine coordinate for either end position on $E$, read using its signed residue differential.
  - Explanation: Regular rational families carry coordinates in which the logarithmic two-form is the standard symplectic form.
- **lemma-buffered-seams** (theorem, [source](#stmt-deg-seams)): There are fixed positive parameter and seam widths and a fixed bound on translation derivatives with the following property. Choose $d$ sufficiently small, then $\alpha\neq0$ sufficiently small depending on $d$. At matching nominal end positions the normalized core and neck charts have degree-one symplectic transitions of the form $$F_j=J_j\circ(\operatorname{id}+E_j),\qquad J_j(w,p)=(w+a_j(p),p),$$ on buffered seams. After choosing long-coordinate origins and cut heights, they satisfy the domain hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#sew:chain" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sew:chain">27</a>, apart from exactness, and $$\sup_j\\|E_j\\|\leq C d+\epsilon(d,\alpha), \qquad \epsilon(d,\alpha)\longrightarrow0 \quad(\alpha\to0, d\text{ fixed}).$$ The widths and the bound for $a_j'$ can be chosen before $d$.
  - Explanation: Core and neck charts meet on buffered seams by degree-one symplectic transitions that are small perturbations of shears.
- **thm-rational-direction-open** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rat-open)): For every primitive positive integral vector $v\in\Lambda$, there is a nonempty relatively open subset $\mathcal O_v\subset\mathcal D_v$ such that every marked K3 surface with period in $\mathcal O_v$ has property $L$ at every point.
  - Explanation: For each primitive positive integral vector there is a relatively open set of periods containing it, all of whose surfaces have property L everywhere.
- **lemma-isotrivial-center** (construction, [source](#stmt-rat-center)): For every primitive $v\in\Lambda$ of square $2d>0$, there is a marked projective K3 surface $X_0$ whose period contains $v$ and which admits a genus-one fibration $$\pi:X_0\longrightarrow\mathbb P^1$$ with all smooth fibers of $j$-invariant zero. Its primitive fiber class $e\in\Lambda$ satisfies $(e,v)=0$.
  - Explanation: A central projective K3 surface with an isotrivial genus-one fibration whose fiber class is orthogonal to the given vector.
- **lemma-safe-cycles** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rat-safe)): The forms $\eta_\gamma$ and functions $h_\gamma$ extend to $B$. The map $$h=(h_{\gamma_1},h_{\gamma_2}):B\longrightarrow(\mathbb R/\mathbb Z)^2$$ has finite fibers. For each primitive $\gamma$, put $$F_\gamma=h_\gamma(B\setminus B^\circ).$$ Outside a finite subset of $B$, at least two of the three cycles $\gamma_1,\gamma_2,\gamma_1+\gamma_2$ have action outside their respective finite sets $F_\gamma$. Any two of these cycles form an integral homology basis.
  - Explanation: Action forms extend across punctures, the action map has finite fibers, and safe cycle choices exist outside a finite set.
- **lemma-uniform-strips-deformation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rat-strips)): Fix compact families of regular torus charts on $X_0$, each equipped with a safe primitive cycle, and suppose their actions are separated from the corresponding forbidden values by a positive margin. Prescribe any finite number of period cells in these initial data. For all sufficiently small $t\in\mathcal D_v$, each datum gives a symplectic immersed strip on $X_t$, periodic in its long coordinate with period $1$, of positive transverse width. On the prescribed initial cells its chart is arbitrarily close in $C^1$ to the original cylinder chart. All widths and closeness requirements can be chosen uniformly over the given compact data.
  - Explanation: Safe torus charts deform to periodic symplectic strips of uniform positive width on nearby surfaces.
- **lemma-two-cycles-distinct-leaves** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rat-two-periods)): In the setting of Lemma <a href="#rat:strips" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rat:strips">38</a>, suppose two initial strips are obtained from independent primitive cycles of one central smooth torus. One can choose a finite required initial block length and a positive $C^1$ error tolerance from the central torus and its two cycles, before restricting the deformation parameter, with the following property. For strips meeting these bounds, if $$\label{rat:destroy-fiber} ([\sigma_t],e)\ne0,$$ then $L$ holds at every point of the smaller tube covered by both initial strip charts.
  - Explanation: When the period pairing with the fiber class is nonzero, strips from independent cycles have distinct leaves and property L holds on the covered tube.
- **lemma-orbit-density** (theorem, [source](#stmt-dyn-orbits)): Let $P\in\mathcal D$.* 1. *If $P\cap(\Lambda\otimes\mathbb Q)=\{0\}$, its integral-isometry orbit is dense in $\mathcal D$.* 2. *If $P\cap(\Lambda\otimes\mathbb Q)=\mathbb Qv$ for a primitive integral vector $v$, its orbit under integral isometries fixing $v$ is dense in $\mathcal D_v$.
  - Explanation: Orbits of periods with no rational direction are dense in the period domain; with one rational direction they are dense in the corresponding fixed-vector locus.
- **cor-oka-one-interpolation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-oka1)): Let $S$ be a complex K3 surface, $R$ an open Riemann surface, $K\subset R$ a compact Runge set, and $A=\{a_j:j\geq1\}\subset R$ a closed discrete set of distinct points. Let $k_j\geq1$ be integers. For every continuous map $h:R\to S$ holomorphic near $K\cup A$ and every $\epsilon>0$, there is a holomorphic map $f:R\to S$, homotopic to $h$, satisfying $$\sup_{z\in K}d_S(f(z),h(z))<\epsilon, \qquad j_{a_j}^{k_j}f=j_{a_j}^{k_j}h\quad(j\geq1).$$ Finite or empty interpolation sets are also allowed. If each prescribed first derivative is nonzero, $f$ can be chosen to be an immersion.
  - Explanation: Approximation with jet interpolation from open Riemann surfaces into K3 surfaces within a homotopy class, immersive for nonzero first derivatives.
- **cor-surface-classification** (classification, [source](#stmt-cor-surface-classification)): Every complex Enriques surface is Oka. More generally, every connected minimal compact complex surface of Kodaira dimension zero is Oka. A connected minimal compact complex surface of class VII is Oka if and only if it is a Hopf surface or an Enoki surface.
  - Explanation: Enriques surfaces and all minimal compact surfaces of Kodaira dimension zero are Oka; among minimal class VII surfaces exactly the Hopf and Enoki surfaces are.
- **cor-global-spray** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-global-spray)): Every projective complex K3 surface and every complex Enriques surface is elliptic in Gromov’s sense.
  - Explanation: Projective K3 and Enriques surfaces carry a global dominating spray.

        "exact_quote": "Suppose there are holomorphic maps $$\\sigma_1:\\mathbb C\\times\\Delta_b\\longrightarrow X,\\qquad\n\\sigma_2:\\Delta_b\\times\\mathbb C\\longrightarrow X$$ which are locally biholomorphic at $(0,0)$ and satisfy $\\sigma_1(z,0)=\\sigma_2(z,0)$ for small $z$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-loc-pieces",
        "exact_quote": "Given a special pair $D\\subset D'$ and an open neighborhood $N$ of $D$, there are compact sets $A,B_0,B_1\\subset\\mathbb C$ and bounded open sets $U_A,U_0,U_1$ such that $$\\begin{gathered}\n\\overline{U_A}\\subset\\operatorname{int}A,\\qquad\n\\overline{U_i}\\subset\\operatorname{int}B_i\\quad(i=0,1),\\\\\nD\\subset U_0,\\qquad D'\\subset U_A\\cup U_0\\cup U_1,\\\\\nA\\cap D=\\varnothing,\\qquad B_0\\cap B_1=\\varnothing,\\qquad B_0\\subset N.\n\\end{gathered}$$ The compact sets $D\\cup A$ and $B_0\\cup B_1$ are polynomially convex. For $U_B=U_0\\cup U_1$, the two closed fringes $\\overline{U_A\\setminus U_B}$ and $\\overline{U_B\\setminus U_A}$ have positive distance.",
        "source_id": "PM-042.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-loc-splitting",
        "exact_quote": "Let $U_A,U_B\\subset\\mathbb C$ be bounded open sets with positively separated closed fringes, and put $C=U_A\\cap U_B$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-loc-alignment",
        "exact_quote": "Two strip maps locally biholomorphic at chosen meeting preimages, with transverse central curves there, can be put in the form of Proposition <a href=\"#loc:criterion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"loc:criterion\">4</a>. Locally lift the first central curve through the inverse of the second strip, writing the lift as $(a(z),t(z))$, where its bounded coordinate is $a$. Replace the second strip by $\\widetilde\\sigma_2(z,w)=\\sigma_2(a(z),t(z)+w)$. Transversality says $a'(0)\\ne0$, and time zero gives exact axis agreement. If the original second central curve also passes through $x$ and its strip is regular there, some finite $c$ gives a regular chart $\\widetilde\\sigma_2(0,c)=x$. For finite-length families whose central germs converge to transverse germs through a common point, the implicit function theorem first gives nearby intersection points and meeting preimages. Translate the preimages to the origin and apply the same construction. Convergence and transversality give a uniformly positive bounded-coordinate radius; the translations and the function $t$ lose only a bounded amount of long-coordinate length. Thus this construction also supplies the aligned families in the finite-length part of the proposition.",
        "source_id": "PM-042.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-loc-alignment",
          "exact_quote": "Two strip maps locally biholomorphic at chosen meeting preimages, with transverse central curves there, can be put in the form of Proposition <a href=\"#loc:criterion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"loc:criterion\">4</a>. Convergence and transversality give a uniformly positive bounded-coordinate radius; the translations and the function $t$ lose only a bounded amount of long-coordinate length.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-loc-gluing",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose two holomorphic sprays over bounded planar domains $U_A,U_B$ and a parameter polydisc are uniformly close on their overlap, with a positive margin in an auxiliary spray polydisc. Suppose the first spray is a chart spray there with uniform inverse-chart margins, and the closed planar fringes are positively separated. If the closeness is sufficiently small, their central maps can be glued after arbitrarily small holomorphic corrections in the auxiliary variables. The glued map is arbitrarily close to the first central map on any prescribed compact subset of its domain on which that spray is fixed, or has a uniform modulus of continuity in its auxiliary variables.",

        "exact_quote": "The proof also records a useful uniform version: finitely many coordinate discs whose boundaries lie in fixed compact subsets of the good locus retain the required boundary containment after sufficiently small deformations of the coordinate charts.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-glue-open",
        "exact_quote": "In the preceding buffered open-overlap geometry, let two holomorphic sprays into a complex manifold be related on their overlap by a fiber coordinate change $$\\gamma(z,v)=v+c(z,v),\\qquad v\\in B_b.$$ Suppose $\\|c\\|_\\infty$ can be made arbitrarily small with $b>0$ fixed. After any prescribed positive loss of base and fiber margins, the two sprays admit fiber reparametrizations close to the identity which make them agree. Evaluating at $v=0$ gives a glued holomorphic map. It is arbitrarily close to the original map on each compactum where the corresponding input spray is fixed, or has a uniform modulus of continuity in the fiber variable. No such uniform target estimate is asserted on a side whose spray varies without this control.",
        "source_id": "PM-042.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-glue-open",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ana-concentration",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $m\\geq2$, numbers $$1<r_m<\\cdots<r_2<r_1,\n \\qquad 0<a<1,\\qquad r_j<a r_{j-1}\\quad(2\\leq j\\leq m).$$ For an integer $d\\geq2$, set $$P_j(w)=(w/r_j)^d,\\qquad\n H_d(x)=\\bigl(a x_m,P_2(x_2)-a x_1,\\ldots,\n                        P_m(x_m)-a x_{m-1}\\bigr).$$ There are constants $C\\geq1$, $q\\in(0,1)$, and $d_0$, depending only on the displayed fixed data, with the following properties. For $d\\geq d_0$, $N\\geq1$, and $$E_{d,N}=H_d^{-N}(\\overline\\Delta_1^{\\,m}),$$ one has $$\\label{ana:eq:power-bound}\n \\max_{j\\geq2}|P_j(x_j)|\\leq C\\max(1,|x_1|)\n \\qquad(x\\in E_{d,N}).$$ On $E_{d,N}\\cap\\{|x_1|>r_1\\}$, all $x_j$ are nonzero and $$\\label{ana:eq:ratio-bound}\n \\left|\\frac{H_d(x)_j}{a x_{j-1}}\\right|\\leq q\n \\qquad(2\\leq j\\leq m).$$ Consequently the holomorphic functions $$\\xi_j(x)=\\log\\left(\\frac{P_j(x_j)}{a x_{j-1}}\\right),\n \\qquad 2\\leq j\\leq m,$$ using the logarithm on $\\{|\\zeta-1|<1\\}$, satisfy $$|\\xi_j(x)|\\leq B:=-\\log(1-q).$$ Here the assertions about holomorphicity are on any open inverse image of the unit polydisc; all the estimates also hold on its closure. With $$D=d^{m-1},\\qquad\n \\rho_1=1,\\qquad \\rho_j=r_j(a\\rho_{j-1})^{1/d},$$ there is a single-valued holomorphic function $s$ on this end such that $$\\label{ana:eq:parameterization}\n x_j=\\rho_j s^{d^{m-j}}\n       \\exp\\left(\\sum_{k=2}^j\\frac{\\xi_k}{d^{j-k+1}}\\right),\n \\qquad 1\\leq j\\leq m.$$ For $j=1$, the sum is empty and this says $x_1=s^D$.",
        "source_id": "PM-042.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ana-concentration",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $m\\geq2$, numbers $$1<r_m<\\cdots<r_2<r_1,\n \\qquad 0<a<1,\\qquad r_j<a r_{j-1}\\quad(2\\leq j\\leq m).$$ For an integer $d\\geq2$, set $$P_j(w)=(w/r_j)^d,\\qquad\n H_d(x)=\\bigl(a x_m,P_2(x_2)-a x_1,\\ldots,\n                        P_m(x_m)-a x_{m-1}\\bigr).$$ There are constants $C\\geq1$, $q\\in(0,1)$, and $d_0$, depending only on the displayed fixed data, with the following properties. For $d\\geq d_0$, $N\\geq1$, and $$E_{d,N}=H_d^{-N}(\\overline\\Delta_1^{\\,m}),$$ one has $$\\label{ana:eq:power-bound}\n \\max_{j\\geq2}|P_j(x_j)|\\leq C\\max(1,|x_1|)\n \\qquad(x\\in E_{d,N}).$$ On $E_{d,N}\\cap\\{|x_1|>r_1\\}$, all $x_j$ are nonzero and $$\\label{ana:eq:ratio-bound}\n \\left|\\frac{H_d(x)_j}{a x_{j-1}}\\right|\\leq q\n \\qquad(2\\leq j\\leq m).$$ Consequently the holomorphic functions $$\\xi_j(x)=\\log\\left(\\frac{P_j(x_j)}{a x_{j-1}}\\right),\n \\qquad 2\\leq j\\leq m,$$ using the logarithm on $\\{|\\zeta-1|<1\\}$, satisfy $$|\\xi_j(x)|\\leq B:=-\\log(1-q).$$ Here the assertions about holomorphicity are on any open inverse image of the unit polydisc; all the estimates also hold on its closure. With $$D=d^{m-1},\\qquad\n \\rho_1=1,\\qquad \\rho_j=r_j(a\\rho_{j-1})^{1/d},$$ there is a single-valued holomorphic function $s$ on this end such that $$\\label{ana:eq:parameterization}\n x_j=\\rho_j s^{d^{m-j}}\n       \\exp\\left(\\sum_{k=2}^j\\frac{\\xi_k}{d^{j-k+1}}\\right),\n \\qquad 1\\leq j\\leq m.$$ For $j=1$, the sum is empty and this says $x_1=s^D$.",
          "source_id": "PM-042.1"

      "exact_statement": "Fix $m\\geq2$, numbers $$1<r_m<\\cdots<r_2<r_1,\n \\qquad 0<a<1,\\qquad r_j<a r_{j-1}\\quad(2\\leq j\\leq m).$$ For an integer $d\\geq2$, set $$P_j(w)=(w/r_j)^d,\\qquad\n H_d(x)=\\bigl(a x_m,P_2(x_2)-a x_1,\\ldots,\n                        P_m(x_m)-a x_{m-1}\\bigr).$$ There are constants $C\\geq1$, $q\\in(0,1)$, and $d_0$, depending only on the displayed fixed data, with the following properties.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ana-polydisc",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#ana:cap\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ana:cap\">14</a>, a map holomorphic near a closed polydisc in $\\mathbb C^m$ can be approximated uniformly there by entire maps $\\mathbb C^m\\to Y$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-glue-interval",
        "exact_quote": "Let $I\\subset\\mathbb R$ be a compact interval and let $C\\subset\\mathbb C^k$ be a compact convex set. Suppose $H_s(q)$ is a smooth family, for $s$ in a neighborhood of $I$, of holomorphic maps on a common neighborhood of $C$, with values in a complex surface. Smoothness is understood on a common neighborhood with all finite derivatives under consideration. For every finite $j$ and every $\\epsilon>0$, after an arbitrarily small loss of the specified compact margins, $H$ can be approximated in $C^j(I\\times C)$ by a map holomorphic on a neighborhood of $I\\times C$ in $\\mathbb C\\times\\mathbb C^k$. The size of this complex neighborhood may depend on the approximant. Additional closed polydisc factors can be included in $C$, with fixed larger neighborhoods.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-glue-linear-trace",
        "exact_quote": "There are $C>0$ and an integer $p\\geq1$ with the following property. For $0<h<\\min(r/2,1)$ and a vector-valued function $u$ on $M_r\\times B_b$ with the regularity just specified, there are linear operators producing functions $a^\\pm$, holomorphic on $Q_{r-h}^\\pm\\times B_b$ and continuous up to $M_{r-h}\\times B_b$, such that $$a^+-a^-=u\\quad\\hbox{on }M_{r-h}\\times B_b,\n \\qquad\n \\|a^+\\|_{r-h,b,*}+\\|a^-\\|_{r-h,b,*}\n       \\leq Ch^{-p}\\sup_{v\\in B_b}\\|u(\\,\\cdot\\,,v)\\|_{C^\\alpha(M_r)}.$$ The operators preserve holomorphic dependence on all auxiliary variables.",
        "source_id": "PM-042.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-glue-linear-trace",
          "exact_quote": "There are $C>0$ and an integer $p\\geq1$ with the following property. For $0<h<\\min(r/2,1)$ and a vector-valued function $u$ on $M_r\\times B_b$ with the regularity just specified, there are linear operators producing functions $a^\\pm$, holomorphic on $Q_{r-h}^\\pm\\times B_b$ and continuous up to $M_{r-h}\\times B_b$, such that $$a^+-a^-=u\\quad\\hbox{on }M_{r-h}\\times B_b,\n \\qquad\n \\|a^+\\|_{r-h,b,*}+\\|a^-\\|_{r-h,b,*}\n       \\leq Ch^{-p}\\sup_{v\\in B_b}\\|u(\\,\\cdot\\,,v)\\|_{C^\\alpha(M_r)}.$$ The operators preserve holomorphic dependence on all auxiliary variables.",
          "source_id": "PM-042.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are $C>0$ and an integer $p\\geq1$ with the following property. For $0<h<\\min(r/2,1)$ and a vector-valued function $u$ on $M_r\\times B_b$ with the regularity just specified, there are linear operators producing functions $a^\\pm$, holomorphic on $Q_{r-h}^\\pm\\times B_b$ and continuous up to $M_{r-h}\\times B_b$, such that $$a^+-a^-=u\\quad\\hbox{on }M_{r-h}\\times B_b,\n \\qquad\n \\|a^+\\|_{r-h,b,*}+\\|a^-\\|_{r-h,b,*}\n       \\leq Ch^{-p}\\sup_{v\\in B_b}\\|u(\\,\\cdot\\,,v)\\|_{C^\\alpha(M_r)}.$$ The operators preserve holomorphic dependence on all auxiliary variables.",
      "explanation": "Additive splitting of a Hölder jump across a real interface with polynomial loss in the margin.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prj-strips",
        "exact_quote": "Let $S$ be a smooth projective complex K3 surface. There is a nonempty Zariski open subset $U\\subset S$ such that, for every $x\\in U$, there are $b>0$ and holomorphic maps $$\\sigma_1:\\mathbb C\\times\\Delta_b\\longrightarrow S,\\qquad\n\\sigma_2:\\Delta_b\\times\\mathbb C\\longrightarrow S$$ which are locally biholomorphic at the origin and satisfy $$\\sigma_1(0,0)=\\sigma_2(0,0)=x,\\qquad\n\\sigma_1(z,0)=\\sigma_2(z,0)\\quad (|z|<b).$$ The complement $S\\setminus U$ is algebraic of dimension at most one. Consequently $S$ has property $L$ everywhere.",
        "source_id": "PM-042.1"
      },

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prj-strips",

Consequently $S$ has property $L$ everywhere.",
          "source_id": "PM-042.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prj-prepare",
        "exact_quote": "Let $S$ be a smooth projective integral surface, let $H$ be an ample divisor, and let $p:Y\\to T$ be a smooth proper family of connected genus-one curves over a smooth irreducible algebraic curve. Suppose that $q:Y\\to S$ has nonconstant source-curve moduli and that $q_{t_0}$ is the normalization map of an integral curve $C\\subset S$. After removing finitely many points of $T$, every $q_t$ normalizes an integral image curve $C_t$ with $H\\cdot C_t=H\\cdot C$. The evaluation map $q$ is dominant and generically finite, and some nonempty Zariski open $V\\subset S$ has finite etale surjective inverse image map $q^{-1}(V)\\to V$.",
        "source_id": "PM-042.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-sew-chain",
        "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be a complex surface with a nowhere-zero holomorphic two-form $\\omega$. Fix $0<h<1/8$, $r>0$, and $A<\\infty$. For each $j\\in\\mathbb Z$, let $L_j\\geq1$ and let $$\\phi_j:\\{ -2h<Y<L_j+2h\\}\\times\\Delta_r\\longrightarrow M$$ be a holomorphic map with $\\phi_j^*\\omega=\\omega_0$. Suppose there are holomorphic translations $$J_j(w,p)=(w+a_j(p),p),\\qquad\n |\\operatorname{Im}a_j(p)+L_j|<h/8,\\qquad |a_j'(p)|\\leq A,$$ and exact symplectic transitions $F_j$ on $$S_j(h,r)=\\{|Y-L_j|<h\\}\\times\\Delta_r$$ such that $\\phi_j=\\phi_{j+1}\\circ F_j$. Write $$J_j^{-1}F_j=(w+u_j,p+v_j).$$ Assume these lifts exist and $\\sup_j\\|(u_j,v_j)\\|_{S_j(h,r)}\\leq e$.*\n\n*There are $b>0$ and $e_*>0$, depending only on $h,r,A$ and the indicated margins, such that, if $e<e_*$, these charts give a holomorphic symplectic immersion $$\\Phi:\\mathbb C\\times\\Delta_b\\longrightarrow M$$ which is periodic of period one in the first variable. On a smaller domain in every original block, with fixed positive margins at both ends, $\\Phi$ is obtained from $\\phi_j$ by a small periodic symplectic change of variables. The corrected transitions are $$(w,p)\\longmapsto(w+\\widetilde a_j(p),p).$$ The changes of variables and $\\widetilde a_j-a_j$, together with any fixed finite number of derivatives on further smaller domains, tend uniformly to zero as $e\\to0$. None of these assertions requires an upper bound for the $L_j$ or a bound on the number of blocks.",

      },

Suppose there are holomorphic translations $$J_j(w,p)=(w+a_j(p),p),\\qquad\n |\\operatorname{Im}a_j(p)+L_j|<h/8,\\qquad |a_j'(p)|\\leq A,$$ and exact symplectic transitions $F_j$ on $$S_j(h,r)=\\{|Y-L_j|<h\\}\\times\\Delta_r$$ such that $\\phi_j=\\phi_{j+1}\\circ F_j$. Write $$J_j^{-1}F_j=(w+u_j,p+v_j).$$ Assume these lifts exist and $\\sup_j\\|(u_j,v_j)\\|_{S_j(h,r)}\\leq e$.*\n\n*There are $b>0$ and $e_*>0$, depending only on $h,r,A$ and the indicated margins, such that, if $e<e_*$, these charts give a holomorphic symplectic immersion $$\\Phi:\\mathbb C\\times\\Delta_b\\longrightarrow M$$ which is periodic of period one in the first variable.         "anchor": "stmt-sew-linearize",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $E=(w+u,p+v)$ is exact symplectic on a seam and $\\|(u,v)\\|\\leq e$.         "anchor": "stmt-sew-split",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose the periodic functions $H_j$ have mean zero, are holomorphic on $S_j(s,\\rho)$, and have norm at most $e$. Their bounds, on domains with the appropriate additional losses, are $$\\|G_j\\|\\leq C\\delta^{-1}e,\\qquad\n \\|X_{G_j}\\|\\leq C\\delta^{-2}e,\\qquad\n \\|DX_{G_j}\\|\\leq C\\delta^{-3}e.$$ All constants are uniform in the block lengths and the number of blocks.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sew-centers",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose symplectically normalized charts, together with corresponding model charts, are available for centers varying in larger transverse discs. Suppose the actual charts are uniformly close to their models, and transitions at matching model positions are uniformly close to degree-one shears. The transition into a newly added block can be made exact by a transverse translation of that block of size bounded by its transition error. Recenter its model by the same translation. The approximation of that block by its own recentered model retains its original bound.*\n\n*Consequently, such adjustments can be repeated indefinitely whenever the updated centers remain in a region with uniform buffered chart choices. No bound on their accumulated displacement relative to the first center is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-042.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "There are fixed positive parameter and seam widths and a fixed bound on translation derivatives with the following property. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Property L** ([source](#stmt-loc-property)): A complex surface $X$ has property $L$ at $x$ if there is an open neighborhood $V$ of $x$ with the following property. For every special pair $D\subset D'$, every pair of closed polydiscs $P_0\Subset\operatorname{int}P\subset\mathbb C^q$, and every holomorphic map $f$ from a neighborhood of $D\times P$ into $V$, the restriction $f\|_{D\times P_0}$ is a uniform limit of maps holomorphic near $D'\times P_0$ with values in $X$. The case $q=0$ is included. We call $V$ a *witness neighborhood*.
- **Special pair** ([source](#sec:local)): A *special pair* $D\subset D'$ consists of two closed topological discs with piecewise smooth boundary, where $D'$ is obtained from $D$ by attaching another such disc along a proper boundary arc.
- **Disc of radius r** ([source](#sec:local)): Write $\Delta_r=\{z\in\mathbb C:\|z\|<r\}$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-pieces-splitting** [claim](#stmt-loc-splitting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-loc-splitting): Cutoff function and Cauchy-Green integral operator -> bounded linear splitting operators
- **step-two-strips-to-L** [claim](#stmt-loc-criterion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-loc-splitting-2): Oka-Weil polynomials plus contraction mapping with the splitting operators -> property L from two complete directions
- **step-concentration** [claim](#stmt-ana-concentration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ana-concentration): Forward-invariant cone and logarithmic recursion for the polynomial automorphism -> parameterization by a planar variable and bounded transverse variables
- **step-polydisc** [claim](#stmt-ana-polydisc) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ana-polydisc): Planar enlargement with passive parameters, then gluing on a buffered overlap -> approximation on polydiscs
- **step-convex-induction** [claim](#stmt-ana-cap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-glue-trace-2): Induction removing one real face at a time using interface gluing -> convex approximation property
- **step-projective-strips** [claim](#stmt-prj-strips) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prj-flow-2): Complete vertical flows of two genus-one families -> property L on projective K3 surfaces
- **step-chain-sewing** [claim](#stmt-sew-chain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sew-split-2): Quadratic Newton iteration with summable margin losses -> periodic symplectic immersion of a strip
- **step-rational-open** [claim](#stmt-rat-open) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rat-two-periods-2): Finite preparation, deformation of strips, disc propagation -> open set of periods with property L
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-dyn-orbits-2): Case split on the rational dimension of the period plane, orbit density and Torelli -> every K3 surface has property L, hence is Oka

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-cap-every-k3**: Every complex K3 surface has the convex approximation property in every source dimension: maps holomorphic near a nonempty compact convex set are uniform limits on that set of entire maps into the surface.
- **inv-local-property-implies-cap**: For a complex surface with a complete compatible distance, the local operation L holding at every point implies the convex approximation property in every source dimension.
- **inv-dense-prescribed-jet-immersion**: Through every point of a complex K3 surface and every nonzero tangent vector there passes a holomorphic immersion of the complex line with that value and first derivative and with dense image in the ordinary topology.
- **inv-exact-symplectic-sewing**: An infinite chain of cylindrical symplectic charts with exact transitions close to degree-one shears sews into one periodic holomorphic symplectic immersion of a strip, with no bound on block lengths or on the number of blocks.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-higher-dimensional-targets**: Apply the approximation theorem or its local-to-global criterion to target manifolds of complex dimension above two. -> The criterion is formulated and proved only for complex surfaces; nothing is asserted for other targets.
- **anti-non-exact-sewing**: Sew annular chart chains whose transitions are not exact symplectic. -> A closed holomorphic one-form on an overlap can carry a nonzero period, which blocks the iteration from converging.
- **anti-uniform-off-compact-set**: Expect the approximating entire map to stay close to the given map outside the compact convex set, or on unbounded domains. -> The estimate is a supremum over the compact set only; no control of the distance elsewhere is stated.
- **anti-numeric-orbit-search**: Turn the dense-orbit step on the period domain into a computational search for a good period. -> The statement gives density of an orbit, an existence assertion with no procedure or effective bound.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are existence statements in complex analysis and K3 period theory: approximation by entire maps, sewing of infinite chains, and density of orbits of arithmetic groups obtained via Ratner's orbit closure theorem. No finite procedure with explicit inputs and outputs is stated, and no computable bound is given, so no executable reference implementation or oracle is meaningful.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **lim-complex-surface-target** (hypothesis): The local-to-global criterion is stated only for a complex surface with a complete compatible distance. Nothing is asserted for targets of higher complex dimension. Blocked use: ['applying the criterion to Calabi-Yau threefolds or other higher-dimensional targets', 'dropping completeness of the distance when exhausting by polydiscs']
- **lim-compact-convex-source** (edge_case): Approximation is asserted only on a nonempty compact convex source set inside the domain of the given map. No control on the distance between the entire map and the given map is stated away from that set. Blocked use: ['expecting uniform closeness on unbounded or non-convex subsets', 'using the entire map as a global replacement of the local map']
- **lim-exact-symplectic-transitions** (hypothesis): The sewing theorem needs exact symplectic transitions. A closed holomorphic one-form on an annulus can have a nonzero period, which obstructs the iteration, so non-exact transitions must first be recentered. Blocked use: ['sewing annular chains with non-exact transitions directly', 'assuming the period vanishes without checking exactness']
- **lim-zariski-density-projective** (formal_scope): Zariski density of the entire curve is stated only for projective K3 surfaces. For general K3 surfaces only density in the ordinary topology is claimed. Blocked use: ['claiming Zariski density on nonprojective K3 surfaces']
- **lim-global-spray-projective-only** (formal_scope): Ellipticity in Gromov's sense is concluded only for projective K3 surfaces and Enriques surfaces. Nothing is asserted about a global dominating spray on a nonprojective K3 surface. Blocked use: ['inferring a global dominating spray on every K3 surface from the Oka property']
- **lim-nonconstructive-period-transfer** (nonconstructive): The step from open regions of periods to every K3 surface uses an orbit closure theorem for unipotent flows. This gives density with no explicit procedure or effective bound. Blocked use: ['extracting an algorithm or running time for the approximating map', 'searching numerically for a good period as a certificate']
- **lim-enriques-scope** (formal_scope): The Oka conclusion for Enriques surfaces is obtained through the K3 double cover and descent along coverings. It does not extend by this argument to arbitrary surfaces outside the stated classes. Blocked use: ['applying the corollary to non-minimal or properly elliptic surfaces']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that every complex K3 surface <i>X</i> is Oka, resolving the K3 Oka conjecture. Equivalently, for every <i>m</i> ≥ 1, every holomorphic map to <i>X</i> from a neighborhood of a compact convex set in ℂ<sup><i>m</i></sup> can be approximated uniformly on that set by entire maps $`\mathbb C^m\to X`$.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

A *complex K3 surface* is a compact connected complex surface that is simply connected and has trivial canonical bundle. Thus it carries a nowhere-zero holomorphic two-form. Every complex K3 surface is Kähler, but it need not be projective or contain any curve (Huybrechts 2016, vol. 158, chap. 1 and 7) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Huybrechts](#ref-Huybrechts)]. This combination of a rigid cohomological structure and potentially sparse algebraic geometry makes K3 surfaces a natural setting in which to study holomorphic flexibility.

The *convex approximation property* (CAP) for a complex manifold asks that maps holomorphic near a compact convex subset of $\mathbb C^m$ can be approximated there by maps defined on all of $\mathbb C^m$, for every source dimension $m$. Forstnerič proved that CAP characterizes Oka manifolds (Forstnerič 2006, 2017) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[ForstnericCAP](#ref-ForstnericCAP), [Forstneric](#ref-Forstneric)]. In its equivalent Stein-source formulation, the Oka property includes deformation of continuous maps to holomorphic maps, with approximation and holomorphic interpolation. Our main result is the following precise approximation statement.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $X$ be a complex K3 surface, with any smooth Hermitian distance $d_X$. For every integer $m\geq1$, every nonempty compact convex set $K\subset\mathbb C^m$, every open neighborhood $U$ of $K$, every holomorphic map $f:U\to X$, and every $\epsilon>0$, there exists a holomorphic map $F:\mathbb C^m\to X$ such that $$\sup_{z\in K}d_X(F(z),f(z))<\epsilon.$$*

</div>

Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> answers the K3 question in (Forstnerič and Lárusson 2011, sec. 8, Problem C) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[ForstnericLarussonSurvey](#ref-ForstnericLarussonSurvey)] and proves the positive conjecture formulated explicitly in (Xie and Zhao 2026, Introduction) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[XieZhao](#ref-XieZhao)]. Its scope includes nonprojective surfaces and imposes no condition on the Picard rank or existence of an elliptic fibration.

An *entire curve* is a holomorphic map from $\mathbb C$, and it is an *immersion* if its derivative never vanishes. The approximation and interpolation theory gives the following concrete consequence, proved in Section <a href="#sec:dense-entire" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:dense-entire">10</a>.

<a id="stmt-cor-dense"></a>
<div id="cor:dense" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2**. *Let $S$ be a complex K3 surface. For every $x\in S$ and every nonzero vector $v\in T_xS$, there is a holomorphic immersion $f:\mathbb C\to S$ such that $$f(0)=x,\qquad f'(0)=v,\qquad \overline{f(\mathbb C)}=S,$$ where closure is taken in the ordinary complex topology. If $S$ is projective, then $f(\mathbb C)$ is Zariski dense.*

</div>

Here the tangent vector, not just its line, is prescribed. For projective $S$, the density conclusion confirms the K3 case of Campana’s prediction of Zariski-dense entire curves on special projective manifolds (Campana 2004) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Campana](#ref-Campana)]. Here specialness means the absence of a meromorphic fibration whose positive-dimensional base, endowed with the orbifold structure induced by the multiple fibers, is of general type. See (Cadorel, Campana, and Rousseau 2022, Conjecture 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[CCR](#ref-CCR)] for the prediction and (Cadorel, Campana, and Rousseau 2022, Proposition 8.9) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[CCR](#ref-CCR)] for the specialness of projective varieties with trivial canonical bundle. The ordinary density and prescribed first jet are additional conclusions.

Section <a href="#sec:surface-consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:surface-consequences">10.2</a> derives the Oka property for Enriques surfaces and hence for all minimal compact complex surfaces of Kodaira dimension zero. It also obtains global dominating sprays on projective K3 and Enriques surfaces. A separate class-VII classification there combines earlier Oka results with the global spherical shell theorem of the companion manuscript (OpenAI 2026) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[OpenAIGSS](#ref-OpenAIGSS)]; that theorem is not an input to the K3 argument.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Earlier results and the remaining obstacles

The use of holomorphic sprays and approximation to realize topological flexibility is central to Gromov’s Oka principle for elliptic bundles (Gromov 1989) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[GromovOka](#ref-GromovOka)]. Forstnerič’s convex approximation characterization (Forstnerič 2006, Theorem 0.1) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[ForstnericCAP](#ref-ForstnericCAP)] makes CAP an effective criterion for this flexibility, while his interpolation theorem (Forstnerič 2005, Corollary 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[ForstnericInterpolation](#ref-ForstnericInterpolation)] links it to prescribed holomorphic data on subvarieties. Our proof uses local chart sprays and splitting arguments in this tradition, with the uniform estimates needed for the strip and convex-face constructions made explicit.

Holomorphic flexibility on K3 surfaces has been studied through entire curves, domination by affine space, and approximation. Buzzard and Lu proved that elliptic and Kummer K3 surfaces are holomorphically dominable by $\mathbb C^2$ (Buzzard and Lu 2000, Propositions 4.4–4.5) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[BuzzardLu](#ref-BuzzardLu)]. Dominability means the existence of a map $\mathbb C^2\to X$ whose differential is surjective somewhere. Forstnerič and Lárusson strengthened this for every Kummer surface to *strong dominability*: such a map can pass through each prescribed target point with surjective differential (Forstnerič and Lárusson 2014, Corollary 3) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[ForstnericLarussonSurfaces](#ref-ForstnericLarussonSurfaces)]. These properties concern individual maps; CAP asks for approximation of every local map in every source dimension.

For arbitrary complex K3 surfaces, Kamenova, Lu and Verbitsky proved that the Kobayashi pseudodistance vanishes (Kamenova, Lu, and Verbitsky 2014, Corollary 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[KamenovaLuVerbitsky](#ref-KamenovaLuVerbitsky)]; the period-dynamics argument is corrected in (Verbitsky 2017, sec. 4) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[VerbitskyErratum](#ref-VerbitskyErratum)]. This removes an intrinsic obstruction to holomorphic flexibility, but does not provide approximation or interpolation.

Alarcón and Forstnerič introduced the Oka-1 property and established it for Kummer surfaces and elliptic K3 surfaces (Alarcón and Forstnerič 2025, Proposition 8.4 and Corollary 8.6) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[AlarconForstneric](#ref-AlarconForstneric)]. Oka-1 concerns approximation and discrete jet interpolation from open Riemann surfaces, within prescribed continuous homotopy classes. Its full formulation is recalled below. The source dimension distinction matters: the one-dimensional property alone does not give Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

For projective K3 geometry, Chen, Gounelas and Liedtke developed existence results for curves of prescribed geometric genus (X. Chen, Gounelas, and Liedtke 2022) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[ChenGounelasLiedtke](#ref-ChenGounelasLiedtke)]. Chen and Gounelas then proved that every projective K3 surface contains genus-one curves of unbounded self-intersection whose smooth normalizations vary in moduli (X. Chen and Gounelas 2022, Theorem A) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[ChenGounelas](#ref-ChenGounelas)]. We use two of their families with different ample degrees. Their complete genus-one fibers supply holomorphic flows for all complex times, and the difference in degrees forces their tangent directions to be generically independent.

More recently, Xie and Zhao proved that smooth $(2,2,2)$ hypersurfaces in $(\mathbb P^1)^3$ are Oka and established dense $G_\delta$ sets of Oka K3 periods and corresponding local deformation parameters (Xie and Zhao 2026, Theorems A, B, and E) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[XieZhao](#ref-XieZhao)]. Their work combines geometric constructions of flexibility with arithmetic dynamics of periods and Torelli theory. We use the same period-theoretic framework, including the corrected orbit alternatives of Verbitsky (Verbitsky 2017) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[VerbitskyErratum](#ref-VerbitskyErratum)], with different geometric constructions. A dense collection of Oka surfaces does not by itself settle the property for each surface: the transfer must address every possible rational subspace of its period plane.

Two analytic obstacles arise even before that transfer. Local approximation in one variable must retain arbitrary holomorphic parameters and then imply approximation on arbitrary convex sets. Moreover, the geometric constructions produce long chains of annuli; errors at their joins must be corrected without exhausting a fixed positive transverse width. We handle the first issue by a polynomial change of source coordinates and gluing estimates measured on real faces. We handle the second by exact symplectic sewing with bounds independent of the chain lengths.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The proof and its organization

The analytic and geometric parts meet at a local operation, denoted $L$. It enlarges a planar disc across an attached disc, for maps with image in a sufficiently small target neighborhood, while retaining any prescribed smaller polydisc of passive holomorphic parameters. Section <a href="#sec:local" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:local">2</a> defines $L$ precisely and obtains it from two strip maps, one defined on $\mathbb C\times\Delta$ and the other on $\Delta\times\mathbb C$, with transverse complete directions. Aligning their coordinate axes exactly makes the overlap error arbitrarily small. A bounded additive splitting and a contraction correct it. The same section propagates $L$ across the center of a small disc whose boundary already lies in the locus where $L$ holds.

Sections <a href="#sec:gluing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:gluing">3</a> and <a href="#sec:globalization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:globalization">4</a> prove that $L$ at every point implies CAP in every dimension (Theorem <a href="#ana:cap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ana:cap">14</a>). A polynomial automorphism concentrates the part of an enlarged polydisc outside the original domain into one long coordinate and bounded transverse coordinates. The planar operation with passive parameters then gives approximation on polydiscs. To pass to an arbitrary convex set, we remove the defining real faces of a containing polytope one at a time. The necessary gluing estimate uses Hölder accuracy on each real face, without requiring a fixed complex thickness on which that accuracy holds.

Section <a href="#sec:projective" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:projective">5</a> constructs the two complete directions on projective K3 surfaces using the Chen–Gounelas families. Propagation across their algebraic exceptional set gives $L$ everywhere. This handles one case of the final period argument and also exhibits a direct source of the required local geometry.

The nonprojective cases require strips on open regions of period space. Section <a href="#sew:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sew:section">6</a> first establishes the uniform sewing theorem. On an annulus, a closed holomorphic one-form can have a nonzero period. Exactness of the symplectic transition removes this obstruction: the transverse constant Fourier coefficient becomes quadratic in the error. The other Fourier modes admit a splitting whose bounds are independent of the number and lengths of the annuli. A quadratic iteration then retains a positive transverse radius along an infinite chain.

To describe the geometric transfer, fix the K3 lattice $\Lambda$, the integral second-cohomology lattice with its intersection form. A *marking* identifies $H^2(X,\mathbb Z)$ with $\Lambda$. The real and imaginary parts of a nonzero holomorphic two-form span an oriented positive two-plane, the *period* of the marked surface. Its rational directions are the vectors in its intersection with $\Lambda\otimes\mathbb Q$.

Section <a href="#deg:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="deg:section">7</a> constructs a nonempty open region in the full period domain on which every surface has $L$ everywhere. It smooths a union of two quadrics and sews annuli along their common elliptic curve. The chart estimates persist under unrestricted small K3 deformations. Section <a href="#rat:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="rat:section">8</a> constructs a second kind of region: for each fixed positive integral vector $v$, a relatively open set among the period planes containing $v$. Here an isotrivial genus-one fibration supplies the central model. Conservation of imaginary symplectic action keeps all recentered annuli away from singular fibers. Two independent cycles supply distinct strip leaves after deformation and hence the transverse directions needed for $L$.

Finally, Section <a href="#dyn:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="dyn:section">9</a> applies Ratner’s orbit theorem and Verbitsky’s corrected rational-direction analysis. Periods with no rational direction have orbits meeting the first open region. Periods with exactly one rational direction have orbits, under its stabilizer, meeting the corresponding second region. Rational period planes give projective K3 surfaces, already treated above. Torelli theory transfers $L$ along these orbits, and Theorem <a href="#ana:cap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ana:cap">14</a> finishes the proof. Section <a href="#sec:dense-entire" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:dense-entire">10</a> derives interpolation, Corollary <a href="#cor:dense" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:dense">2</a>, and the further surface consequences.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
###### Conventions and standard inputs.

Maps on a compact set are always defined on a neighborhood of that set. Approximation may lose any prescribed positive amount of an auxiliary domain margin. A strip means $\mathbb C\times\Delta_b$, with no injectivity requirement unless stated; local regularity is specified at each use. The analytic background consists of Stein theory, scalar approximation and the $L^2$ $\bar\partial$ theorem (Forstnerič 2017; Hörmander 1990; Siu 1976) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Forstneric](#ref-Forstneric), [Hormander](#ref-Hormander), [Siu](#ref-Siu)]. We recall the needed K3 period, Torelli, and nef-pencil results at their applications (Huybrechts 2016, vol. 158, chaps. 2, 6–8, and 11) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Huybrechts](#ref-Huybrechts)]. The arithmetic inputs are likewise stated where used. The local approximation, sewing, geometric constructions, and reductions between them are proved here.

<a id="sec:local"></a>
### Local approximation from complete directions

The immediate goal is an operation that enlarges one planar source variable while retaining arbitrary passive holomorphic parameters. Two transverse complete strip maps provide this operation; a second construction propagates it across points not initially covered by strips. These are the inputs to the convex approximation argument of Section <a href="#sec:globalization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:globalization">4</a>.

Write $\Delta_r=\{z\in\mathbb C:|z|<r\}$. A map on a compact set will always mean a map holomorphic on an open neighborhood of that set. A *special pair* $D\subset D'$ consists of two closed topological discs with piecewise smooth boundary, where $D'$ is obtained from $D$ by attaching another such disc along a proper boundary arc. All approximation assertions use an arbitrary fixed Hermitian distance on the target.

<a id="stmt-loc-property"></a>
<div id="loc:property" class="definition">

**Definition 3**. A complex surface $X$ has property $L$ at $x$ if there is an open neighborhood $V$ of $x$ with the following property. For every special pair $D\subset D'$, every pair of closed polydiscs $P_0\Subset\operatorname{int}P\subset\mathbb C^q$, and every holomorphic map $f$ from a neighborhood of $D\times P$ into $V$, the restriction $f|_{D\times P_0}$ is a uniform limit of maps holomorphic near $D'\times P_0$ with values in $X$. The case $q=0$ is included. We call $V$ a *witness neighborhood*.

</div>

Witness neighborhoods can be shrunk, and the locus where $L$ holds is consequently open.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Planar splitting and two strip maps

In the first strip the first coordinate is complete; in the second strip the second coordinate is complete. Their agreement below is along a germ of the first strip’s complete central curve and a transverse starting section of the second strip, not along both complete central curves. This agreement will make the overlap error small without restricting either complete direction. The conclusion is also allowed at a displaced point on the second strip’s central curve, a feature needed later when two leaves meet tangentially.

<div class="samepage">

<a id="stmt-loc-criterion"></a>
<div id="loc:criterion" class="proposition">

**Proposition 4** (Two complete directions). *Suppose there are holomorphic maps $$\sigma_1:\mathbb C\times\Delta_b\longrightarrow X,\qquad
\sigma_2:\Delta_b\times\mathbb C\longrightarrow X$$ which are locally biholomorphic at $(0,0)$ and satisfy $\sigma_1(z,0)=\sigma_2(z,0)$ for small $z$. If $\sigma_2$ is locally biholomorphic at $(0,c)$, then $L$ holds at $x=\sigma_2(0,c)$.*

*The same conclusion holds for a sequence of pairs whose long coordinate domains are discs of radii tending to infinity, whose bounded-coordinate radii are bounded below, and whose maps converge near $(0,0)$ and, for the second map, near $(0,c)$ to the above nondegenerate germs. Exact axis agreement is required for every pair. In this version $x$ is the value of the limiting second germ at $(0,c)$.*

</div>

</div>

To construct an extension, we will choose a polynomial map $h=(h_1,h_2)$ on the source. On one planar piece only $h_2$ must be small, so that the first strip can be used; on the complementary pieces only $h_1$ must be small, so that the second strip can be used. Both coordinates are small on their overlaps. The next lemma arranges these pieces while keeping the original disc in one of the second-strip pieces.

<a id="stmt-loc-pieces"></a>
<div id="loc:pieces" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5**. *Given a special pair $D\subset D'$ and an open neighborhood $N$ of $D$, there are compact sets $A,B_0,B_1\subset\mathbb C$ and bounded open sets $U_A,U_0,U_1$ such that $$\begin{gathered}
\overline{U_A}\subset\operatorname{int}A,\qquad
\overline{U_i}\subset\operatorname{int}B_i\quad(i=0,1),\\
D\subset U_0,\qquad D'\subset U_A\cup U_0\cup U_1,\\
A\cap D=\varnothing,\qquad B_0\cap B_1=\varnothing,\qquad B_0\subset N.
\end{gathered}$$ The compact sets $D\cup A$ and $B_0\cup B_1$ are polynomially convex. For $U_B=U_0\cup U_1$, the two closed fringes $\overline{U_A\setminus U_B}$ and $\overline{U_B\setminus U_A}$ have positive distance.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-loc-pieces"><summary>Proof of stmt-loc-pieces</summary>

*Proof.* An ambient homeomorphism of the plane takes the attached-disc pair to $D=[-2,0]\times[-1,1]$ and $D'=[-2,3]\times[-1,1]$. Indeed, compatible parametrizations of the attaching arc and the remaining boundary arcs extend over both discs by Jordan–Schoenflies; the resulting outer boundary parametrization extends across the exterior. In this model choose $$0<c<c'<a'<a<b<b'<d'<d<3,\qquad 0<\delta<\epsilon.$$ Use the following horizontal intervals: $$\begin{array}{c|c|c}
&\text{compact piece}&\text{open piece}\\ \hline
B_0,\ U_0&[-2-\epsilon,a]&(-2-\delta,a')\\
A,\ U_A&[c,d]&(c',d')\\
B_1,\ U_1&[b,3+\epsilon]&(b',3+\delta).
\end{array}$$ All compact rectangles have vertical interval $[-1-\epsilon,1+\epsilon]$, and all open rectangles have interval $(-1-\delta,1+\delta)$. Take $a,\epsilon$ small enough that $B_0$ is in the prescribed neighborhood. The closed fringes are disjoint compact sets, as is seen from their horizontal intervals $[a',b']$ and $[-2-\delta,c']\cup[d',3+\delta]$. Pull the sets back by the homeomorphism. Compact containment and positive separation persist. The compact unions in the statement are unions of two disjoint filled Jordan discs, hence have connected complement and are polynomially convex. ◻

</details>

Figure <a href="#fig:pieces" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:pieces">1</a> displays the complementary coordinate control for $h=(h_1,h_2)$: the first coordinate is small on the two outer pieces, and the second is small on the middle piece.

<figure id="fig:pieces">

<figcaption>Horizontal extents of the three compact pieces, shown in separate rows. In the rectangular model, all three pieces share a vertical interval slightly larger than that of $D′$. The two overlap bands control both coordinates.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-pieces"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:pieces`)

- Middle row: two blue-shaded rectangular boxes labeled $B_0$ on the left and $B_1$ on the right, separated by a central gap. To the right is the label "$h_1\text{ small}$". Box $A$ spans the gap and overlaps with the right end of $B_0$ and the left end of $B_1$.

The following is the additive Cousin splitting underlying holomorphic spray gluing; compare (Forstnerič 2003, Lemma 4.6) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[ForstnericSplitting](#ref-ForstnericSplitting)]. We give its integral construction to retain bounds independent of the number and radii of the passive parameters.

<a id="stmt-loc-splitting"></a>
<div id="loc:splitting" class="lemma">

**Lemma 6**. *Let $U_A,U_B\subset\mathbb C$ be bounded open sets with positively separated closed fringes, and put $C=U_A\cap U_B$. For every open parameter polydisc $P$ and every $k$, there are bounded linear operators $$\begin{aligned}
R_A&:H^\infty(C\times P,\mathbb C^k)\longrightarrow
H^\infty(U_A\times P,\mathbb C^k),\\
R_B&:H^\infty(C\times P,\mathbb C^k)\longrightarrow
H^\infty(U_B\times P,\mathbb C^k)
